# Queue

Every open item in rcad, one row each, in five tiers. `docs/decisions.md` §230
is the argument for the shape and the grammar below. The dispatcher edits this
file — to `lane:` when it dispatches a row, to `done:` when it merges one — and
a lane never writes it, so no two lanes conflict over it.

A row is one line inside a fenced block under its tier's heading, as `<id>
<tier> <state> <model> <write set> <what>`, the fields split by spaces. The
shape is only described here, never fenced: `just check-queue` reads every
fenced line as a row, so a fenced template would be a row nobody ranked.

- **id** — `T<n>`, `D<n>` or `Q<n>`, unique in the file. `T` and `D` numbers are
  carried over from the retired `docs/test-plan.md`.
- **tier** — `1`, `2`, `2.5`, `3` or `4` (§230.1).
- **state** — `todo`; `lane:<branch>`; `after:<id>[,<id>]`; `done:<sha>`;
  `dropped:§N`; or `parked:§N`.
- **model** — `opus` or `sonnet`; `-` only on a closed row that never recorded one.
- **write set** — comma-separated paths, directories (ending `/`), or
  `path#item`; `-` means the lane writes no tracked file but a new section of
  its own.
- **what** — ends in at least one `§N`/`§N.M`; on a `done` row the last one is
  the section that records the result.

A row is **ready** when its state is `todo`, or `after:` names only `done` rows.

Q254 1    done:640897036fd2cb9e5f2ba1b89cd8cd6a696c98ea    sonnet  tooling/queue-page/,justfile#queue-page,justfile#check-queue-page-selftest,justfile#gate_steps,justfile#queue,justfile#ff-main   Serve the queue as one HTML page on the tailnet, kept current as a side effect of the queue work. A new recipe, queue-page, reads main's copy of docs/queue.md and the claims file, from whichever worktree runs it, and writes a self-contained page, light and dark, no outside scripts, to ~/rcad-queue-page/index.html through a temporary file and a rename. The queue recipe runs it after every verb that writes, and so does ff-main, which every dispatcher and merger runs after a push; a render that fails says so on stderr and fails neither. The page holds two graphs over time, one point per commit of docs/queue.md on main's first-parent line, with no new state kept beyond a cache: the open queue, split by tier with parked rows as their own band; and the open queue stacked on every row closed so far, so the rise of the closed band is the progress. Below them: ready rows by tier, running rows with their trees, the last closed rows with their sections, and rows added against rows closed per day. check-queue-page-selftest renders a scratch repository's history and checks both graphs' counts at chosen commits, in the gate. Follow the dataviz skill for the charts. Mattias serves the directory himself with tailscale serve --bg ~/rcad-queue-page; this row does not touch Tailscale. §230 §263

Q549 1    done:5d4e60e63eed58a1b17c8fb20dacefacb81d0b28    opus    crates/rcad-topo/src/measure.rs,crates/rcad-topo/tests/a_flat_solid_publishes_the_rounding_of_its_measure.rs   wrong/D The centroid_wobble a flat solid and each flat face publish (§338.5) rests on exact sums that assume nothing underflows, one factor deeper than the volume and area sums of Q402: the solid moment is a determinant times a fourth offset, a face weight is two cross products and its moment takes a fifth side, and the measured middle offset from the hub or apex is scaled down by the axis shift. So a milder mix of coordinate sizes than the volume needs could land a product in the subnormals, make a sum inexact, and publish a wobble smaller than the true distance; nothing detects it, and nobody has run it. Build a solid that reaches it, measure it, and say whether the published wobble still holds the exact middle; if not, charge it (rcad_predicates product_is_exact, an infinite wobble where a product could land in the subnormals), or give the number that puts it outside the §3.5 envelope and re-class the row D. §338.5 §16.10

Build one that way, measure it, and say whether the published errors still hold the exact answer; if not, charge the underflow or refuse by name. §338.3 §16.10

Q560 1    done:d26cb27ab4584dce0fe1b4c5ff99e0f10e690332    opus    crates/rcad-geom/src/cone.rs#point_at,crates/rcad-geom/tests/a_round_trip_through_a_surfaces_own_two_coordinates.rs,crates/rcad-topo/tests/two_frustums_stacked_on_one_cone.rs,docs/decisions.d/357-a-cone-or-a-torus-turned-round-is-one-surface.md   wrong/C Cone::point_at in crates/rcad-geom/src/cone.rs does not undo Cone::coordinates_of where along < 0: it scales the across direction by slope * along, which is negative there, so a point comes back half a turn round from where it was read ((1,0,2) on the cone about -z with slope 0.5 reads (0,-2) and comes back as (-1,0,2), 2.0 away). BuildingAMesh::cut_up in crates/rcad-topo/src/mesh.rs reads a wall boundary with coordinates_of and puts every point the triangulator adds back through point_at, so a frustum whose wall is named about the axis pointing away from it meshes folded (81 of 917 triangles face inward, wall area 78.95 against 21.07) and measures outside the bound it publishes: z in [2, 4] at 12.090699 ± 0.781295 against 14π/3, z in [4, 6] at 35.460664 ± 0.968026 against 38π/3, at deflection 0.01 and 0.001 alike (Q510). Scale by self.radius_at(along) instead. Q510 ran that in a copy: both frustums then hold (14.643758 ± 0.208463, 39.761238 ± 0.348103), their union measures 54.403210 ± 0.558633 against 52π/3 in both orders, and rcad-geom, rcad-mesh, rcad-topo, rcad-step and rcad pass (gate not run).
